<div class='question question'>

Dave and Eddie are cycling around a track. Dave completes a lap every $30\ \text{seconds}$, and Eddie completes a lap every $25\ \text{seconds}$. How many laps will Eddie complete in the time it takes Dave to complete $15$ laps?

- $15$ 
- $16$ 
- $18$ 
- $20$ 
- $25$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total time taken by Dave}     &= 15 \times 30 \\\\
\text{Lapses completed by Eddie}    &= \dfrac {15 \times 30} {25} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {5 \times 3 \times 5 \times 6} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{5} \times 3 \times \cancel{5} \times 6} {\cancel{5} \times \cancel{5}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 6 \\\\
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total time taken by Dave}     &= 15 \times 30 \\\\
\text{Lapses completed by Eddie}    &= \dfrac {15 \times 30} {25} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {5 \times 3 \times 5 \times 6} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{5} \times 3 \times \cancel{5} \times 6} {\cancel{5} \times \cancel{5}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 6 \\\\
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Ann and Ben share $28$ sweets in the ratio $4 : 3$

(Ann has $4$ sweets for every $3$ sweets that Ben has). 

How many sweets do they each receive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sweets received by Ann}                   &= \dfrac {4} {4 + 3} \times 28 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4} {7} \times 28 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 28} {7} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 7 \times 4} {7} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel 7 \times 4} {\cancel 7} \\\\
                                                &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                                                &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ben}                   &= 28 - 16 \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the sweets received by Ann $= a$

Sweets received by Ben $= 28 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 : 3                                           &= a : (28 - a) \\\\
\dfrac{4} {3}                                   &= \dfrac {a} {28 - a} \\\\
4 \times (28 - a)                               &= a \times 3 \\\\
112 - 4a                                        &= 3a \\\\
112                                             &= 3a + 4a \\\\
3a + 4a                                         &= 112 \\\\
7a                                              &= 112 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {112} {7} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 16} {7} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 16} {\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                               &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ann}                   &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ben}                   &= 28 - 16 \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{total}            &:& \text{Ann}                  &:& \text{Ben} \\\\
= & 4 + 3                   &:& 4                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 7                       &:& 4                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 7 \times 4              &:& 4 \times 4                  &:& 3 \times 4 \\\\
= & 28                      &:& 16                          &:& 12
\end{array}
$

Sweets received by Ann $= 16$

Sweets received by Ben $= 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16, 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sweets received by Ann}                   &= \dfrac {4} {4 + 3} \times 28 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4} {7} \times 28 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 28} {7} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 7 \times 4} {7} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel 7 \times 4} {\cancel 7} \\\\
                                                &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                                                &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ben}                   &= 28 - 16 \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the sweets received by Ann $= a$
<emptyline>
Sweets received by Ben $= 28 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 : 3                                           &= a : (28 - a) \\\\
\dfrac{4} {3}                                   &= \dfrac {a} {28 - a} \\\\
4 \times (28 - a)                               &= a \times 3 \\\\
112 - 4a                                        &= 3a \\\\
112                                             &= 3a + 4a \\\\
3a + 4a                                         &= 112 \\\\
7a                                              &= 112 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {112} {7} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 16} {7} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 16} {\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                               &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ann}                   &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ben}                   &= 28 - 16 \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{total}            &:& \text{Ann}                  &:& \text{Ben} \\\\
= & 4 + 3                   &:& 4                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 7                       &:& 4                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 7 \times 4              &:& 4 \times 4                  &:& 3 \times 4 \\\\
= & 28                      &:& 16                          &:& 12
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A necklace is made from black and white beads in a ratio of $3 : 2$, in other words for every $3$ black beads there were $2$ white beads.

$\spadesuit \quad \spadesuit \quad\spadesuit \quad\spadesuit \quad\spadesuit \quad\spadesuit \quad\spadesuit \quad\spadesuit \quad\spadesuit \quad\spadesuit$

If there are $80$ beads altogether, how many beads are black?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of black beads $= a$

Number of white beads $= 80 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{black} : 2 \ \text{white}   &= a \ \text{black} : (80 - a) \ \text{white} \\\\
\dfrac{3} {2}                         &= \dfrac {a} {80 - a} \\\\
3 \times (80 - a)                     &= a \times 2 \\\\
240 - 3a                              &= 2a \\\\
240                                   &= 2a + 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                               &= 240 \\\\
5a                                    &= 240 \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {240} {5} \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {5 \times 48} {5} \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 48} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                     &= 48
\end{aligned}
$

Number of black beads $= 48$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{necklace}                             &:& \text{black beads} \\\\
= & 2 + 3                                       &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5                                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 16                                 &:& 3 \times 16 \\\\
= & 80                                          &:& 48
\end{array}
$

Number of black beads $= 48$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of black beads $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of white beads $= 80 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{black} : 2 \ \text{white}   &= a \ \text{black} : (80 - a) \ \text{white} \\\\
\dfrac{3} {2}                         &= \dfrac {a} {80 - a} \\\\
3 \times (80 - a)                     &= a \times 2 \\\\
240 - 3a                              &= 2a \\\\
240                                   &= 2a + 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                               &= 240 \\\\
5a                                    &= 240 \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {240} {5} \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {5 \times 48} {5} \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 48} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                     &= 48
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of black beads $= 48$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{necklace}                             &:& \text{black beads} \\\\
= & 2 + 3                                       &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5                                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 16                                 &:& 3 \times 16 \\\\
= & 80                                          &:& 48
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of black beads $= 48$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

|    $\spadesuit$           |                           |   $\spadesuit$    |
|    $\spadesuit$           |  $\spadesuit$             |   $\spadesuit$    |
|      Type $A$             |    Type $B$               |   Type $C$        |
| great spotted woodpecker  |lesser spotted woodpecker  | green woodpecker  |


</div>
<div class='workings'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Complete the table below to show the percentages of each type of woodpecker.

|    Type $A$      |    Type $B$      |    Type $C$      |
|:------------:    |:------------:    |:------------:    |
| $\ldots%$        | $\ldots%$        | $\ldots%$        |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of type A woodpecker}      &= 6 \times 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 60000 \\\\
\text{Number of type B woodpecker}      &= 10000 \\\\
\text{Number of type C woodpecker}      &= 3 \times 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 30000 \\\\
\text{Total number of woodpeckers}      &= 60000 + 30000 + 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 100000
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of type A woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {60000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 60 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage of type B woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {10000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 10 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage of type C woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {30000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {30000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {30 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \%, 10 \%, 30 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of type A woodpecker}      &= 6 \times 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 60000 \\\\
\text{Number of type B woodpecker}      &= 10000 \\\\
\text{Number of type C woodpecker}      &= 3 \times 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 30000 \\\\
\text{Total number of woodpeckers}      &= 60000 + 30000 + 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 100000
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of type A woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {60000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 60 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage of type B woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {10000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 10 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage of type C woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {30000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {30000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {30 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Screenwash is used to clean car windows. 

To use Screenwash you mix it with water.

| Winter mixture          | Summer Mixture          |
|:--------------:         |:---------------:        |
| Mix $1$ part Screenwash with $4$ parts water. | Mix $1$ part Screenwash with $9$ parts water. |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In winter, how much water should I mix with $150\ \text{ml}$ of Screenwash?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of water mixed $= \text{a}$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{ml} : 4 \ \text{ml}                   &= 150 \ \text{ml} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {150} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 150 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 600 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Water mixed $= 600 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{screenwash}                           &:& \text{water} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{ml}                               &:& 4 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1 \times 150                                &:& 4 \times 150 \\\\
= & 150 \ \text{ml}                             &:& 600 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Water mixed $= 600 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of water mixed $= \text{a}$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{ml} : 4 \ \text{ml}                   &= 150 \ \text{ml} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {150} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 150 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 600 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Water mixed $= 600 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{screenwash}                           &:& \text{water} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{ml}                               &:& 4 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1 \times 150                                &:& 4 \times 150 \\\\
= & 150 \ \text{ml}                             &:& 600 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Water mixed $= 600 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$600 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In summer, how much Screenwash should I mix with $450\ \text{ml}$ of water?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of water mixed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
9 \ \text{ml} : 1 \ \text{ml}                   &= 450 \ \text{ml} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
9                                               &= \dfrac {450} {a} \\\\
9 \times a                                      &= 450 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {450} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 50} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 50} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Screenwash mixed $= 50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{water}                                &:& \text{screenwash} \\\\
= & 9 \ \text{ml}                               &:& 1 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {1} {9} \\\\
= & 1 \times 450                                &:& \dfrac {450} {9} \\\\
= & 450                                         &:& \dfrac {9 \times 50} {9} \\\\
= & 450                                         &:& \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 50} {\cancel 9} \\\\
= & 450 \ \text{ml}                             &:& 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Screenwash mixed $= 50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>

$50 \ \text{ml}$

</div>

9 \times a                                      &= 450 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {450} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 50} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 50} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 20} {\cancel 5} \\\\

\end{aligned}

<div class='question question'>

$\pounds 50000$ in $\pounds 50$ notes weighs about $1.3 \ \text{Kg}$. How much, in grams, does one $\pounds 50$ note weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of notes}      &= \dfrac {50000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 \times 1000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel{50} \times 1000} {\cancel{50}} \\\\
                            &= 1000 \\\\
\text{Weight of a note}     &= \dfrac {1.3} { 1000} \ \text{kg} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1000} { 1000} \ \text {g} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} { 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0} } \\\\
                            &= 1.3 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.3 \ \text {g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of notes}      &= \dfrac {50000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 \times 1000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel{50} \times 1000} {\cancel{50}} \\\\
                            &= 1000 \\\\
\text{Weight of a note}     &= \dfrac {1.3} { 1000} \ \text{kg} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1000} { 1000} \ \text {g} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} { 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0} } \\\\
                            &= 1.3 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the mass of the alloy is $72 \ \text{kg}$, how much iron and tungsten does it contain?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of iron $= a$

Mass of tungsten $= 72 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{kg} : 1 \ \text{kg}                   &= a \ \text{kg} : (72 - a) \ \text{kg} \\\\
\dfrac{5}{1}                                    &= \dfrac {a} {72 - a} \\\\
5 \times (72 - a)                               &= a \\\\
5 \times 72 - 5a                                &= a \\\\
360 - 5a                                        &= a \\\\
360                                             &= 5a + a \\\\
5a + a                                          &= 360 \\\\
6a                                              &= 360 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {360} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 60} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 60} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 60 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of iron}         &= 60 \ \text{kg} \\\\
\text{Mass of tungsten}     &= 72 - 60 \\\\
                            &= 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{alloy}                  &:& \text{iron}                &:& \text{tungsten} \\\\
= & (5 + 1) \ \text{kg}           &:& 5 \ \text{kg}              &:& 1 \ \text{kg} \\\\
= & 6                             &:& 5                          &:& 1 \\\\
= & 6 \times 12                   &:& 5 \times 12                &:& 1 \times 12 \\\\
= & 72 \ \text{kg}                &:& 60 \ \text{kg}             &:& 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$

Mass of iron $= 60 \ \text{kg}$

Mass of tungsten $= 12 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \ \text{kg}, 12 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the mass of iron $= a$
<emptyline>
Mass of tungsten $= 72 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,

\begin{aligned}

360                                             &= 5a + a \\\\
5a + a                                          &= 360 \\\\
6a                                              &= 360 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {360} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 60} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 60} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 60 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

                            &= 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If there is $15 \ \text{kg}$ of iron in a quantity of the alloy, how much tungsten is there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of tungsten $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{kg} : 1 \ \text{kg}                   &= 15 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{kg} \\\\
5                                               &= \dfrac {15} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 15 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

Tungsten $= 3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{iron}                                 &:& \text{tungsten} \\\\
= & 5 \ \text{kg}                               &:& 1 \ \text{kg} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 15                                 &:& \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
= & 15                                          &:& \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5} \\\\
= & 15                                          &:& \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\
= & 15 \ \text{kg}                              &:& 3 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$

Tungsten $= 3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

\end{array}

<emptyline>

</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Five bags of apples cost $\pounds 3.25$.

How much would seven bags of apples cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of one bag}        &= \pounds \dfrac {3.25} {5} \\\\
\text{Cost of seven bags}     &= 7 \times \dfrac {3.25} {5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 3.25} {5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 325} {5 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 5 \times 65} {5 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel 5 \times 65} {\cancel 5 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {455} {100} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 4.55
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of seven bags of apples $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{apples} : \pounds 3.25                &= 7 \ \text{apples} : \pounds a \\\\
\dfrac {5} {3.25}                               &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 7 \times 3.25 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 3.25} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 325} {5 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 5 \times 65} {5 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel 5 \times 65} {\cancel 5 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {455} {100} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 4.55
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of apples $= \pounds 4.55$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{apples}       &:& \text{cost} \\\\
= & 5                   &:& \pounds 3.25 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {3.25} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 7          &:& \dfrac {3.25 \times 7} {5} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {325 \times 7} {5 \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {5 \times 65 \times 7} {5 \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 65 \times 7} {\cancel 5 \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {455} {100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \pounds 4.55
\end{array}
$

Cost of apples $= \pounds 4.55$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.55$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                              &= \pounds 4.55

$

<div class='uuid'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-5:1:4</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_blocked</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>

<div class='question question'>

A pile of hay can feed $12$ horses for $15 \ \text{days}$.

For how long could the same pile of hay feed $20$ horses? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 15} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5 \times 3} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 15} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5 \times 3} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>1EDD9DF8-7CB7-499A-A11A-243302CCDF2C</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-5:1:7</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

$x$ and $y$ are positive whole numbers. The ratio of $x$ to $y$ is $7:3$. The difference between $x$ and $y$ is $24$.

Find the value of $x$ and the value of $y$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
x - y                                                       &= 24 \\\\
x                                                           &= 24 + y
\end{aligned}
$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
7 \ \text{x} : 3 \ \text{y}                     &= (24 + y) \ \text{x} : y \ \text{y} \\\\
\dfrac{7}{3}                                    &= \dfrac {24 + y} {y} \\\\
7 \times y                                      &= (24 + y) \times 3 \\\\
7y                                              &= 24 \times 3 + 3y \\\\
7y                                              &= 72+ 3y \\\\
7y - 3y                                         &= 72 \\\\
4y                                              &= 72 \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {72} {4} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 18} {4} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 18} {\cancel 4} \\\\
y                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of y to find x.

$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= 24 + y \\\\
            &= 24 + 18 \\\\
            &= 42
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$x= 42,y= 18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x - y                                                       &= 24 \\\\
x                                                           &= 24 + y
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
7 \ \text{x} : 3 \ \text{y}                     &= (24 + y) \ \text{x} : y \ \text{y} \\\\
\dfrac{7}{3}                                    &= \dfrac {24 + y} {y} \\\\
7 \times y                                      &= (24 + y) \times 3 \\\\
7y                                              &= 24 \times 3 + 3y \\\\
7y                                              &= 72+ 3y \\\\
7y - 3y                                         &= 72 \\\\
4y                                              &= 72 \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {72} {4} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 18} {4} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 18} {\cancel 4} \\\\
y                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's use the value of y to find x.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= 24 + y \\\\
            &= 24 + 18 \\\\
            &= 42
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$x= 42,y= 18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>DB9B4354-3AB5-437F-B86E-A69BF2EF462D</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-6:1:6</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_pr</p>
</div>

<div class='topics'>

</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a group of $48$ children, the ratio of boys to girls is $3:5$.

How many boys must join the group to make the ratio of boys to girls $5 : 3?$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of boys before adding new boys $= b$

Number of girls before adding new boys $= 48 - b$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
b \ \text{boys} : (48 - b) \ \text{girls}       &= 3 \ \text{boys} : 5 \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac {b} {48 - b}                             &= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
b \times 5                                      &= 3 \times (48 - b) \\\\
5b                                              &= 3 \times 48 - 3b \\\\
5b                                              &= 144 - 3b \\\\
5b + 3b                                         &= 144 \\\\
8b                                              &= 144 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {144} {8} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 18} {8} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 18} {\cancel 8} \\\\
b                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of boys}       &= 18 \\\\
\text{Number of girls}      &= 48 - 18 \\\\
                            &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{girls}        &:& \text{boys} \\\\
= & 3                   &:& 5 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac{5} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 30} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 3 \times 10} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 3 \times 10} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& 5 \times 10 \\\\
= & 30                  &:& 50
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of boys added}       &= 50 - 18 \\\\
                                  &= 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of boys before adding new boys $= b$
<emptyline>
Number of girls before adding new boys $= 48 - b$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
b \ \text{boys} : (48 - b) \ \text{girls}       &= 3 \ \text{boys} : 5 \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac {b} {48 - b}                             &= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
b \times 5                                      &= 3 \times (48 - b) \\\\
5b                                              &= 3 \times 48 - 3b \\\\
5b                                              &= 144 - 3b \\\\
5b + 3b                                         &= 144 \\\\
8b                                              &= 144 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {144} {8} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 18} {8} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 18} {\cancel 8} \\\\
b                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of boys}       &= 18 \\\\
\text{Number of girls}      &= 48 - 18 \\\\
                            &= 30
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{girls}        &:& \text{boys} \\\\
= & 3                   &:& 5 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac{5} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 30} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 3 \times 10} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 3 \times 10} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& 5 \times 10 \\\\
= & 30                  &:& 50
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of boys added}       &= 50 - 18 \\\\
                                  &= 32
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A pile of food can feed $12$ rabbits for $15 \ \text{days}$.

For how long could the same pile of food feed $20$ rabbits? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 15} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5 \times 3} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 15} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5 \times 3} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

In a class the ratio of boys to girls is $3 {:} 5$.

If there are $6$ less boys than girls, how any pupils are in the class in total? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of girls $= g$

Number of boys $= g - 6$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
(g - 6) \ \text{boys} : g \ \text{girls}        &= 3 \ \text{boys} : 5 \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac {g - 6} {g}                              &= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
(g - 6) \times 5                                &= 3 \times g \\\\
5g - 30                                         &= 3g \\\\
5g - 3g                                         &= 30 \\\\
2g                                              &= 30 \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {30} {2} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 15} {2} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 15} {\cancel 2} \\\\
g                                               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of girls}      &= 15 \\\\
\text{Number of boys}       &= 15 - 6 \\\\
                            &= 9 \\\\
\text{Total pupils}         &= 15 + 9 \\\\
                            &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of girls $= g$
<emptyline>
Number of boys $= g - 6$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$

(g - 6) \times 5                                &= 3 \times g \\\\
5g - 30                                         &= 3g \\\\
5g - 3g                                         &= 30 \\\\
2g                                              &= 30 \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {30} {2} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 15} {2} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 15} {\cancel 2} \\\\
g                                               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

\begin{aligned}

                            &= 9 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question subquestion'>

How many bricks will there be in the wall?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total bricks}                                 &= 23 \times 12 \\\\
                                                    &= 276
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='answers'>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

She can lay $6$ bricks every $4 \ \text{minutes}$. How long will it take her to build the wall?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time to build the wall}                       &= \dfrac {4} {6} \times 276 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 276} {6} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 6 \times 46} {6} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel{6} \times 46} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
                                                    &= 4 \times 46 \\\\
                                                    &= 184 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                                    &= 3 \ \text{hours} \ 4 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \text{ hours } 4 \text{ minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 276} {6} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 6 \times 46} {6} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel{6} \times 46} {\cancel{6}} \\\\
                                                    &= 4 \times 46 \\\\
                                                    &= 184 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                                    &= 3 \ \text{hours} \ 4 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>F4EA0A59-8C6E-47D9-8760-31CB473DCE39</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2010--sample-paper-1:1:11</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>

tratio

</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Bella makes purple paint by mixing blue paint and red paint in the ratio of $4 : 3$. How much blue paint is needed to make $21 \ \text{litres}$ of purple paint?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of blue paint $= b$

Volume of red paint $= 21 - b$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
b \ \text{litres} : (21 - b) \ \text{litres}       &= 4 \ \text{litres} : 3 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac {b} {21 - b}                                &= \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
3 \times b                                         &= 4 \times (21 - b) \\\\
3b                                                 &= 84 - 4b \\\\
3b + 4b                                            &= 84 \\\\
7b                                                 &= 84 \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {84} {7} \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {7 \times 12} {7} \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 12} {\cancel 7} \\\\
b                                                  &= 12 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Blue paint needed $= 12 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple}                                &:& \text{blue} \\\\
= & (4 + 3) \ \text{litres}                      &:& 4 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 7                                            &:& 4 \\\\
= & 7 \times 3                                   &:& 4 \times 3 \\\\
= & 21 \ \text{litres}                           &:& 12 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Blue paint needed $= 12 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of blue paint $= b$
<emptyline>
Volume of red paint $= 21 - b$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
b \ \text{litres} : (21 - b) \ \text{litres}       &= 4 \ \text{litres} : 3 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac {b} {21 - b}                                &= \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
3 \times b                                         &= 4 \times (21 - b) \\\\
3b                                                 &= 84 - 4b \\\\
3b + 4b                                            &= 84 \\\\
7b                                                 &= 84 \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {84} {7} \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {7 \times 12} {7} \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 12} {\cancel 7} \\\\
b                                                  &= 12 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Blue paint needed $= 12 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple}                                &:& \text{blue} \\\\
= & (4 + 3) \ \text{litres}                      &:& 4 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 7                                            &:& 4 \\\\
= & 7 \times 3                                   &:& 4 \times 3 \\\\
= & 21 \ \text{litres}                           &:& 12 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Blue paint needed $= 12 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

James draws a plan of his school using a scale of $1 \ \text{cm}$ to $6 \ \text{m}$. On the plan the playground is $5 \ \text{cm}$ long. What is the real length of the playground? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the real length of playground $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{cm} : 6 \ \text{m}                    &= 5 \ \text{cm} : a \ \text{m} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {6}                                  &= \dfrac {5} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 30 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

Real length of playground $= 30 \ \text{m}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{plan}         &:& \text{playground} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{cm}       &:& 6 \ \text{m} \\\\
= & 5                   &:& 6 \times 5 \\\\
= & 5 \ \text{cm}       &:& 30 \ \text{m}
\end{array}
$

Real length of playground $= 30 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the real length of playground $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

\dfrac {1} {6}                                  &= \dfrac {5} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 5 \times 6 \\\\
a                                               &= 30 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Real length of playground $= 30 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{plan}         &:& \text{playground} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{cm}       &:& 6 \ \text{m} \\\\

= & 5 \ \text{cm}       &:& 30 \ \text{m}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

She has $12 \ \text{litres}$ of red paint. How much blue paint will she need? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of blue paint she needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{litres} : 5 \ \text{litres}           &= 12 \ \text{litres} : a \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac {4} {5}                                  &= \dfrac {12} {a} \\\\
4 \times a                                      &= 12 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Blue paint needed $= 15 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{red}          &:& \text{blue} \\\\
= & 4 \ \text{litres}   &:& 5 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac{5} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 12         &:& \dfrac{5 \times 12} {4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 4 \times 3} {4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& 5 \times 3 \\\\
= & 12 \ \text{litres}  &:& 15 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Blue paint needed $= 15 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15 \text{ litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of blue paint she needed $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{litres} : 5 \ \text{litres}           &= 12 \ \text{litres} : a \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac {4} {5}                                  &= \dfrac {12} {a} \\\\
4 \times a                                      &= 12 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Blue paint needed $= 15 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{red}          &:& \text{blue} \\\\
= & 4 \ \text{litres}   &:& 5 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac{5} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 12         &:& \dfrac{5 \times 12} {4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 4 \times 3} {4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& 5 \times 3 \\\\
= & 12 \ \text{litres}  &:& 15 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Blue paint needed $= 15 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15 \text{ litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

How much purple paint will that make? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}

                          &= 27 \text{ litres}

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Eliot needs $36 \ \text{litres}$ of purple paint. How much red paint will he need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of red paint $= a$

Volume of blue paint $= 36 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{litres} : 5 \ \text{litres}           &= a \ \text{litres} : (36 - a) \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac {4} {5}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {36 - a} \\\\
4 \times (36 - a)                               &= 5 \times a \\\\
4 \times 36 - 4a                                &= 5a \\\\
144 - 4a                                        &= 5a \\\\
144                                             &= 5a + 4a \\\\
5a + 4a                                         &= 144 \\\\
9a                                              &= 144 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {144} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 16} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 16} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 16 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Red paint needed $= 16 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple}             &:& \text{red} \\\\
= & (4 + 5) \ \text{litres}   &:& 4 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 9                         &:& 4 \\\\
= & 9 \times 4                &:& 4 \times 4 \\\\
= & 36 \ \text{litres}        &:& 16 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Red paint needed $= 16 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16 \text{ litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of red paint $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{litres} : 5 \ \text{litres}           &= a \ \text{litres} : (36 - a) \ \text{litres} \\\\
\dfrac {4} {5}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {36 - a} \\\\
4 \times (36 - a)                               &= 5 \times a \\\\
4 \times 36 - 4a                                &= 5a \\\\
144 - 4a                                        &= 5a \\\\
144                                             &= 5a + 4a \\\\
5a + 4a                                         &= 144 \\\\
9a                                              &= 144 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {144} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 16} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 16} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 16 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Red paint needed $= 16 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple}             &:& \text{red} \\\\
= & (4 + 5) \ \text{litres}   &:& 4 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 9                         &:& 4 \\\\
= & 9 \times 4                &:& 4 \times 4 \\\\
= & 36 \ \text{litres}        &:& 16 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Red paint needed $= 16 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On the plan, the garden is $2  \ \text{cm}$ wide. What is the real width of the garden?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the actual width of garden $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{cm} : 12 \ \text{m}                   &= 2 \ \text{cm} : a \ \text{m} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {12}                                 &= \dfrac {2} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= 24 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

Real width of the garden $= 24 \ \text{m}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{plan}         &:& \text{garden} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{cm}       &:& 12 \ \text{m} \\\\
= & 1 \times 2          &:& 12 \times 2 \\\\
= & 2 \ \text{cm}       &:& 24 \ \text{m}
\end{array}
$

Real width of the garden $= 24 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2015--sample-paper-6:1:8</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

tratio

<div class='question question'>

'Purple Fire' paint is made by mixing red and blue paint in the ratio $3:1$. If I want to make $1 \ \text{litre}$ of 'Purple Fire' paint, how much red paint do I need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of red paint (in ml) $= a$

Volume of blue paint $= 1000 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{ml} : 1 \ \text{ml}                   &= a \ \text{ml} : (1000 - a) \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {1}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {1000 - a} \\\\
3 \times (1000 - a)                             &= a \\\\
3000 - 3a                                       &= a \\\\
3000                                            &= a + 3a \\\\
a + 3a                                          &= 3000 \\\\
4a                                              &= 3000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3000} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 750} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 750} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 750 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Red paint needed $= 750 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple}                               &:& \text{red} \\\\
= & (1 + 3) \ \text{ml}                         &:& 3 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 4                                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 4 \times 250                                &:& 3 \times 250 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{ml}                            &:& 750 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{litre}                            &:& 750 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Red paint needed $= 750 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$750 \ \text{ml}$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of red paint (in ml) $= a$
<emptyline>
Volume of blue paint $= 1000 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{ml} : 1 \ \text{ml}                   &= a \ \text{ml} : (1000 - a) \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {1}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {1000 - a} \\\\
3 \times (1000 - a)                             &= a \\\\
3000 - 3a                                       &= a \\\\
3000                                            &= a + 3a \\\\
a + 3a                                          &= 3000 \\\\
4a                                              &= 3000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3000} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 750} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 750} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 750 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Red paint needed $= 750 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple}                               &:& \text{red} \\\\
= & (1 + 3) \ \text{ml}                         &:& 3 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 4                                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 4 \times 250                                &:& 3 \times 250 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{ml}                            &:& 750 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{litre}                            &:& 750 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Red paint needed $= 750 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$750 \ \text{ml}$ 
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the cost of one ticket.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 ticket}     &= \dfrac {21.25} {17} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {2125} {17 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {17 \times 125} {17 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {17} \times 125} {\cancel {17} \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {125} {100} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 1.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.25$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>04DE9CD4-9DBE-46AD-B14F-B7A255ECDF40</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-7:1:10</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>

<div class='topics'>

tratio

</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A drink contains lime, orange and apple juices in the ratio $2:7:6$. Find the volume of orange juice contained in $300 \ \text{ml}$ of the drink.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of orange juice $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
(2 + 7 + 6) \ \text{ml} : 7 \ \text{ml}         &= 300 \ \text{ml} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {2 + 7 + 6} {7}                          &= \dfrac {300} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {15} {7}                                 &= \dfrac {300} {a} \\\\
15 \times a                                     &= 300 \times 7 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {300 \times 7} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 15 \times 7} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {15} \times 7} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
a                                               &= 20 \times 7 \\\\
a                                               &= 140 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Orange juice $= 140 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{drink}              &:& \text{orange juice} \\\\
= & (2 + 7 + 6) \ \text{ml}   &:& 7 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 15                        &:& 7 \\\\
= & 15 \times 20              &:& 7 \times 20 \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{ml}           &:& 140 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Orange juice $= 140 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$140 \text { ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of orange juice $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(2 + 7 + 6) \ \text{ml} : 7 \ \text{ml}         &= 300 \ \text{ml} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {2 + 7 + 6} {7}                          &= \dfrac {300} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {15} {7}                                 &= \dfrac {300} {a} \\\\
15 \times a                                     &= 300 \times 7 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {300 \times 7} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 15 \times 7} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {15} \times 7} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
a                                               &= 20 \times 7 \\\\
a                                               &= 140 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Orange juice $= 140 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{drink}              &:& \text{orange juice} \\\\
= & (2 + 7 + 6) \ \text{ml}   &:& 7 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 15                        &:& 7 \\\\
= & 15 \times 20              &:& 7 \times 20 \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{ml}           &:& 140 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Orange juice $= 140 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$140 \text { ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much water should be mixed with $30 \ \text{ml}$ of cordial?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the water mixed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{ml} : 4 \ \text{ml}                   &= 30 \ \text{ml} : a \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {30} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 30 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of water $= 120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cordial}                          &:& \text{water} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{ml}                           &:& 4 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1 \times 30                             &:& 4 \times 30 \\\\
= & 30 \ \text{ml}                          &:& 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Amount of water $= 120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the water mixed $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,

1 \times a                                      &= 30 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of water $= 120 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>

= & 1 \ \text{ml}                           &:& 4 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 1 \times 30                             &:& 4 \times 30 \\\\
= & 30 \ \text{ml}                          &:& 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much cordial should be used to make $750 \ \text{ml}$ of the drink?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let cordial used $= a$

Water used $= 750 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{ml} : 4 \ \text{ml}                   &= a \ \text{ml} : (750 - a) \ \text{ml} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {750 - a} \\\\
750 - a                                         &= 4a \\\\
750                                             &= 4a + a \\\\
4a + a                                          &= 750 \\\\
5a                                              &= 750 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {750} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 150} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 150} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 150 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Cordial used $= 150 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{drink}                            &:& \text{cordial} \\\\
= & (1 + 4) \ \text{ml}                     &:& 1 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 1 \\\\
= & 5 \times 150                            &:& 1 \times 150 \\\\
= & 750 \ \text{ml}                         &:& 150 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Cordial used $= 150 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>6D99174B-5E2A-4872-9160-90380BFA07FA</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-10:1:8</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>

<div class='rag'>

tratio

</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a fruit yoghurt weighing $117 \ \text{g}$, the ratio of weight of fruit to weight of yoghurt is $2:7$. Calculate the weight of fruit.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of fruit $= a$

Weight of yoghurt $= 117 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{g} : 7 \ \text{g}                     &= a \ \text{g} : (117 - a) \ \text{g} \\\\
\dfrac {2} {7}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {117 - a} \\\\
2 \times (117 - a)                              &= 7a \\\\
234 - 2a                                        &= 7a \\\\
234                                             &= 7a + 2a \\\\
2a + 7a                                         &= 234 \\\\
9a                                              &= 234 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {234} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 26} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 26} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 26 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Weight of fruit $= 26 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fruit yoghurt}                        &:& \text{fruit} \\\\
= & (2 + 7) \ \text{g}                          &:& 2 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 9                                           &:& 2 \\\\
= & 9 \times 13                                 &:& 2 \times 13 \\\\
= & 117 \ \text{g}                              &:& 26 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Weight of fruit $= 26 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$26 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of fruit $= a$
<emptyline>
Weight of yoghurt $= 117 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{g} : 7 \ \text{g}                     &= a \ \text{g} : (117 - a) \ \text{g} \\\\
\dfrac {2} {7}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {117 - a} \\\\
2 \times (117 - a)                              &= 7a \\\\
234 - 2a                                        &= 7a \\\\
234                                             &= 7a + 2a \\\\
2a + 7a                                         &= 234 \\\\
9a                                              &= 234 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {234} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 26} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 26} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 26 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Weight of fruit $= 26 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}

= & 9                                           &:& 2 \\\\
= & 9 \times 13                                 &:& 2 \times 13 \\\\
= & 117 \ \text{g}                              &:& 26 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Weight of fruit $= 26 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
