<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>44F0E0BB-97E5-4A28-BD0C-66D1683C7133</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-10:1:9</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A bottle of blackcurrant cordial makes enough drink to fill $60$ glasses when it is diluted in the ratio $1$ part cordial to $4$ parts water. 
How many glasses of drink would a bottle of cordial make if it is diluted in the ratio $1$ part cordial to $5$ parts water?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of glasses of cordial used $= a$

Number of glasses of water used $= 60 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1  : 4                                          &= a  : 60 - a  \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {60 - a} \\\\
60 - a                                          &= 4a \\\\
60                                              &= 4a + a \\\\
4a + a                                          &= 60 \\\\
5a                                              &= 60 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {60} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 12} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

Let's calculate now how many glasses of drink we can have with new ratios.

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cordial}                          &:& \text{drink} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& (1 + 5) \\\\
= & 1 \times 12                             &:& 6 \times 12 \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 72
\end{array}
$

Number of glasses of drink $= 72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of glasses of cordial used $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of glasses of water used $= 60 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
1  : 4                                          &= a  : 60 - a  \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {60 - a} \\\\
60 - a                                          &= 4a \\\\
60                                              &= 4a + a \\\\
4a + a                                          &= 60 \\\\
5a                                              &= 60 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {60} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 12} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's calculate now how many glasses of drink we can have with new ratios.
<emptyline>
Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cordial}                          &:& \text{drink} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& (1 + 5) \\\\
= & 1 \times 12                             &:& 6 \times 12 \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 72
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of glasses of drink $= 72$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$72$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

William changes $\pounds 240$ into Dollars at the beginning of his holiday. How many Dollars does he receive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of dollars he received $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 3 : \text{\textdollar} 5                &= \pounds 240 : \text{\textdollar} a \\\\
\dfrac{3} {5}                                   &= \dfrac {240} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 240 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {240 \times 5} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {80 \times 3 \times 5} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {80 \times \cancel 3 \times 5} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 80 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \text{\textdollar} 400
\end{aligned}
$

Dollars received $= \text{\textdollar} 400$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \pounds                                 &:& \text{\textdollar} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 5 \\\\
= & 3 \times 80                             &:& 5 \times 80 \\\\
= & \pounds 240                             &:& \text{\textdollar} 400
\end{array}
$

Dollars received $= \text{\textdollar} 400$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text{\textdollar} 400$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\end{aligned}

a                                               &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\

\end{aligned}

= & 195                                      &:& 3 \times 39  \\\\

\end{array}

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>74769273-0862-4A60-84DF-948A3F1CC2E6</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>st-albans-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-1:1:18</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Pairs of avocados are sold in a shop. $16$ pairs of avocados are sold at a total cost of $\pounds 24$. The shopkeeper now decides to separate the pairs and sell the avocados individually, but without any change to the total price. What is the cost of an individual avocado? Give your answer in pence.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pair of avocados}     &= \pounds \dfrac {24} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {16} \ \text{pence} \\\\
\text{Cost of 1 avocado}              &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {24 \times 100} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {2 \times 16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 2 \times 2 \times 25} {2 \times 8 \times 2} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel{8} \times 3 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{2} \times 25} {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{8} \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
                                      &= 3 \times 25  \\\\
                                      &= 75 \ \text{pence}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \ \text{pence}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pair of avocados}     &= \pounds \dfrac {24} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {16} \ \text{pence} \\\\
\text{Cost of 1 avocado}              &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {24 \times 100} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {2 \times 16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 2 \times 2 \times 25} {2 \times 8 \times 2} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel{8} \times 3 \times \cancel{2} \times \cancel{2} \times 25} {\cancel{2} \times \cancel{8} \times \cancel{2}} \\\\
                                      &= 3 \times 25  \\\\
                                      &= 75 \ \text{pence}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='papername'>
<p>st-albans-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-1:1:26</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='question question'>

Jason and Patrick win a prize of $\pounds 75$ for their entry into an inventing competition. For every $2 \ \text{hours}$ that Patrick worked on the project Jason worked $3$, so it is decided that for every $\pounds 2$ that Patrick receives Jason should receive $\pounds 3$. How much will Jason and Patrick receive? Write your 
answers in the boxes below. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the money received by Jason $= a$

Money received by Patrick $= 75 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 2 : \pounds 3          &= \pounds (75 - a) : \pounds a \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3}                 &= \dfrac {75 - a} {a} \\\\
2a                             &= 3 \times (75 - a) \\\\
2a                             &= 225 - 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                        &= 225 \\\\
5a                             &= 225 \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {225} {5} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {5 \times 45} {5} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 45} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                              &= \pounds 45
\end{aligned}
$


Money received by Jason $= \pounds 45$

Money received by Patrick $= 75 - 45 = \pounds 30$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{Total}                &:& \text{Patrick}              &:& \text{Jason} \\\\
= & \pounds (2 + 3)             &:& \pounds 2                   &:& \pounds 3 \\\\
= & 5                           &:& 2                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 15                 &:& 2 \times 15                 &:& 3 \times 15 \\\\
= & \pounds 75                  &:& \pounds 30                  &:& \pounds 45
\end{array}
$


Money received by Jason $= \pounds 45$

Money received by Patrick $= \pounds 30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Jason $=$ $\pounds 45$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Patrick $=$ $\pounds 30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the money received by Jason $= a$
<emptyline>
Money received by Patrick $= 75 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 2 : \pounds 3          &= \pounds (75 - a) : \pounds a \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3}                 &= \dfrac {75 - a} {a} \\\\
2a                             &= 3 \times (75 - a) \\\\
2a                             &= 225 - 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                        &= 225 \\\\
5a                             &= 225 \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {225} {5} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {5 \times 45} {5} \\\\
a                              &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 45} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                              &= \pounds 45
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

Money received by Jason $= \pounds 45$
<emptyline>
Money received by Patrick $= 75 - 45 = \pounds 30$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{Total}                &:& \text{Patrick}              &:& \text{Jason} \\\\
= & \pounds (2 + 3)             &:& \pounds 2                   &:& \pounds 3 \\\\
= & 5                           &:& 2                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 15                 &:& 2 \times 15                 &:& 3 \times 15 \\\\
= & \pounds 75                  &:& \pounds 30                  &:& \pounds 45
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>

Money received by Jason $= \pounds 45$
<emptyline>
Money received by Patrick $= \pounds 30$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On a ferry to France, I wanted to buy a large bar of Toblerone 
chocolate, which cost $\pounds 3$. How many Euros would I have to pay for 
it?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the euros $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1 : 1.2 \ \text{Euros}                  &= \pounds 3 : a \ \text{Euros} \\\\
\dfrac{1}{1.2}                                  &= \dfrac {3} {a} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 1.2 \\\\
a                                               &= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}
\end{aligned}
$

Payment $= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \pounds                 &:& \text{Euros} \\\\
= & 1                       &:& 1.2 \\\\
= & 1 \times 3              &:& 1.2 \times 3 \\\\
= & \pounds 3               &:& 3.6 \ \text{Euros}
\end{array}
$

Payment $= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3.6 \ \text{Euro}$

</div>
</div>

a                                               &= 3 \times 1.2 \\\\
a                                               &= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Payment $= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}$
</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \pounds                 &:& \text{Euros} \\\\
= & 1                       &:& 1.2 \\\\
= & 1 \times 3              &:& 1.2 \times 3 \\\\
= & \pounds 3               &:& 3.6 \ \text{Euros}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Payment $= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}$
</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A certain bottle of wine in France costs $7.5 \ \text{Euros}$. How much is 
that in English pounds?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the pounds $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1 : 1.2 \ \text{Euros}                  &= \pounds a : 7.5 \ \text{Euros} \\\\
\dfrac{1}{1.2}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {7.5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7.5} {1.2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {75} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 25} {3 \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 25} {\cancel{3} \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {25} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 6.25
\end{aligned}
$

Cost $= \pounds 6.25$

</div>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \pounds                                                 &:& \text{Euros} \\\\
= & 1                                                       &:& 1.2 \\\\
= & \dfrac {1} {1.2}                                        &:& 1 \\\\
= & \dfrac {7.5} {1.2}                                      &:& 1 \times 7.5 \\\\
= & \dfrac {75} {12}                                        &:& 7.5 \\\\
= & \dfrac {3 \times 25} {3 \times 4}                       &:& 7.5 \\\\
= & \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 25} {\cancel{3} \times 4}     &:& 7.5 \\\\
= & \pounds 6.25                                            &:& 7.5 \ \text{Euros}
\end{array}
$

Cost $= \pounds 6.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 6.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the pounds $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1 : 1.2 \ \text{Euros}                  &= \pounds a : 7.5 \ \text{Euros} \\\\
\dfrac{1}{1.2}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {7.5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7.5} {1.2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {75} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 25} {3 \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 25} {\cancel{3} \times 4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {25} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 6.25
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost $= \pounds 6.25$
</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}

= & \dfrac {\cancel{3} \times 25} {\cancel{3} \times 4}     &:& 7.5 \\\\
= & \pounds 6.25                                            &:& 7.5 \ \text{Euros}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Cost $= \pounds 6.25$
</code></pre>

                        &= \dfrac{\cancel{7} \times 8.30 }{\cancel{7}} \\\\
                        &= \pounds 8.30
\end{aligned}
$

\end{aligned}

<div class='question question'>

David is $15 \ \text{stone} \ 11$. Andrew is $275 \ \text{pounds}$. If there are $14 \ \text{pounds}$ in $1 \ \text{stone}$ what is the difference between their weights? Give your answer in pounds. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}           &= 275 \text{ pounds} - 15 \text{ stone } 11 \text{ pounds} \\\\
                            &= 275 \text{ pounds} - 15 \times 14 \text{ pounds} - 11 \text{ pounds} \\\\
                            &= 275 \text{ pounds} - 15 \times 14 \text{ pounds} - 11 \text{ pounds} \\\\
                            &= 275 - 210 - 11 \\\\
                            &= 54 \text{ pounds}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$54 \text{ pounds}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}           &= 275 \text{ pounds} - 15 \text{ stone } 11 \text{ pounds} \\\\
                            &= 275 \text{ pounds} - 15 \times 14 \text{ pounds} - 11 \text{ pounds} \\\\
                            &= 275 \text{ pounds} - 15 \times 14 \text{ pounds} - 11 \text{ pounds} \\\\
                            &= 275 - 210 - 11 \\\\
                            &= 54 \text{ pounds}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$54 \text{ pounds}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='uuid'>
<p>4C42DA9B-C12A-4A7A-B493-F806C22437DB</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>st-pauls-girls-school--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-1:1:17</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>

<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a country dance there are $3$ boys and $2$ girls in every line. 

$42$ boys take part in the dance. How many girls take part?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of girls $= g$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{boys} : 3 \ \text{girls}              &= 42 \ \text{boys} : g \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac{2}{3}                                    &= \dfrac {42} {g} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {42 \times 3} { 2} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {21 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {21 \times \cancel{2} \times 3} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
g                                               &= 63
\end{aligned}
$

Number of girls $= 63$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{boys}     &:& \text{girls} \\\\
= & 2               &:& 3  \\\\
= & 2 \times 21     &:& 3 \times 21  \\\\
= & 42              &:& 63
\end{array}
$

Number of girls $= 63$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$63 \ \text{girls}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of girls $= g$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{boys} : 3 \ \text{girls}              &= 42 \ \text{boys} : g \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac{2}{3}                                    &= \dfrac {42} {g} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {42 \times 3} { 2} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {21 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
g                                               &= \dfrac {21 \times \cancel{2} \times 3} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
g                                               &= 63
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of girls $= 63$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{boys}     &:& \text{girls} \\\\
= & 2               &:& 3  \\\\
= & 2 \times 21     &:& 3 \times 21  \\\\
= & 42              &:& 63
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of girls $= 63$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$63 \ \text{girls}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Alex has $9$ rangos and $4$ fangos. Barbara has $7$ rangos and $3$ fangos. How many fangos do they both have in total?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total fangos} &= 9 \ \text{rangos} + 4 \ \text{fangos} + 7 \ \text{rangos} + 3 \ \text{fangos} \\\\
                     &= 9 \times 6 \ \text{fangos} + 4 \ \text{fangos} + 7 \times 6 \ \text{fangos} + 3 \ \text{fangos} \\\\
                     &= 9 \times 6 + 4 + 7 \times 6 + 3 \\\\
                     &= 54 + 4 + 42 + 3 \\\\
                     &= 103 \ \text{fangos}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$103  \ \text{fangos}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total fangos} &= 9 \ \text{rangos} + 4 \ \text{fangos} + 7 \ \text{rangos} + 3 \ \text{fangos} \\\\
                     &= 9 \times 6 \ \text{fangos} + 4 \ \text{fangos} + 7 \times 6 \ \text{fangos} + 3 \ \text{fangos} \\\\
                     &= 9 \times 6 + 4 + 7 \times 6 + 3 \\\\
                     &= 54 + 4 + 42 + 3 \\\\
                     &= 103 \ \text{fangos}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$103  \ \text{fangos}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of a bango do $12$ fangos represent?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 12 \ \text{fangos} \\\\
&= \dfrac {12} {6 \times 15} \ \text{bangos} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {6 \times 15} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel 6 \times 15} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2} {15} \ \text{bangos}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {15}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 12 \ \text{fangos} \\\\
&= \dfrac {12} {6 \times 15} \ \text{bangos} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {6 \times 15} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 6} {\cancel 6 \times 15} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2} {15} \ \text{bangos}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {15}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many whole bangos are there in $274$ fangos? How many fangos are left over?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 274 \ \text{fangos} \\\\
&= \dfrac {274} {6 \times 15} \ \text{bangos} \\\\
&= \dfrac {274} {90} \\\\
&= 3 \text{ remainder } 4 \\\\
&\approx 3
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total bangos}         &= 3 \\\\
\text{Left over bangos}     &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3, 4$

</div>

&= \dfrac {274} {90} \\\\
&= 3 \text{ remainder } 4 \\\\
&\approx 3
\end{aligned}
$

\begin{aligned}

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

How many bags of crisps at $16 \ \text{p}$ each can I buy for $\pounds 2 \text{?}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of crisp bags}    &= \dfrac {\text {Total money}} {\text {Money per bag}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\pounds 2} {16 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {200} {16} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {25 \times 8} {2 \times 8} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {25 \times \cancel{8}} {2 \times \cancel{8}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {25} {2} \\\\
                                &= 12 \ \text {remainder} \ 1 \\\\
                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of crisp bags}    &= \dfrac {\text {Total money}} {\text {Money per bag}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\pounds 2} {16 \ \text{p}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {200} {16} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {25 \times 8} {2 \times 8} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {25 \times \cancel{8}} {2 \times \cancel{8}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {25} {2} \\\\
                                &= 12 \ \text {remainder} \ 1 \\\\
                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>
